## CURRENT HANDOFF — patch0145 failure localized; patch0146 compile pending CI (2026-10-28)

Commit `238fca8fac956e7a2476880c3d27fc1abd91377e` passed GitHub Actions run [40673788050](https://github.com/localhosts-A/ios-sweet/actions/runs/40673788050): XNU build, SWEET diagnostics, buildtree packaging, and artifact collection succeeded. Candidate files are under `shim/out-dali/ufs-40673788050/candidate/`. Kernel SHA-256 is `8d45ee87a9f4d8556806bfb6da2fbef6191bee22053a3447a4d964de2e590f72`; `dali-boot.img` SHA-256 is `ddfbc184acfc2c255e356a8583335f1e9a8f90835296d47ace83422871446d39`; `dali-init_boot.img` SHA-256 is `797670200a2a4193d8f30c4e9ac3a539562fe8931a672a5db493ed38506472e9`. The boot image reuses the unchanged kernel-only shim; init_boot contains the new ramoops mirror and UFS failure-stage diagnostics.

The approved trial's panic photo shows `IOFindBSDRoot: SWEET UFS root ufsroot0 unavailable: 6 stage=hci-ready` at `IOKitBSDInit.cpp:817`. Readback of `/sys/fs/pstore/console-ramoops-0` produced 6,114 bytes (SHA-256 `c4aeddba5395dbf680dc5f6b0fa5e883801dee1a7d97b1a4120b0f5b268ad26d`) and confirms HCE/HCS, interrupts and transfer/task queue state were zero. Thus XNU failed before UIC, SCSI, READ CAPACITY, GPT, or media writes; the old assumption that LK left HCI enabled was wrong. The 16 GiB HFSX root image remains installed and readback-verified in `unixnu_data` (SHA-256 `ea6fe9549adc6a2f4f466079e5fb4e2b7432e80977923dbc98a3c6944c5be3cd`). The local D22AP `/product` ADT input is absent, so the image is a kernel/UFS-root diagnostic, not full iOS userspace.

After that trial, Fastboot restored the saved original `init_boot_b` and `boot_b`. Android is back on Dali / 25060RK16C / slot `_b`, `boot_completed=1`; physical hashes match originals exactly: boot_b `6dc40de74fa7de4f435c513047c51fe10634c1891666031039c52a08b27a3cd5`, init_boot_b `08794a432befcf0504ee4933a51260cb6ccc4785b69f345c4b5968e8e6053d2d`. `unixnu_data` remains HFS+ `iOSRoot`; the SWEET range in `unixnu_logs` remains zero with SHA-256 `06955cde7f98b9503653b906e9634732d59a17a6dc2cb1a0e453fbdd4adaab86`.

This patch0145 XNU panic's `console-ramoops-0` survived the specific Fastboot recovery path and was read back from Android. Retention remains path-dependent: an earlier recovery chain yielded empty pstore, while a HackIOS AEE-reset chain retained console-ramoops through delayed manual Fastboot; the empty case's loss stage is unknown. Patch0146 now contains a stopped-HCI-only MT6991 startup path, bounded HCE/UIC/link polls, strict AO/IE/queue checks, and stage-specific panic diagnostics. It exactly replays against the 40673788050 source and `sweet_ufs.c` compiles locally; full CI is pending. No 0146 image has been built or flashed, and it needs its own exact-pair approval before a trial.

## PREVIOUS HANDOFF — Dali internal UFS root/log adaptation prepared (2026-10-28)

Read-only ADB inspection confirms `/dev/block/sdc` is UFS host0 target0 LUN2 at `0x16810000`, with 4096-byte logical/physical blocks. `userdata` is sdc95 (start 27,633,920 / size 421,844,672 Linux 512-byte sectors); the new `unixnu_data` is sdc97 (start 449,478,592 / size 67,043,328 sectors = 31.96875 GiB); `unixnu_logs` is sdc98 (start 516,521,920 / size 65,536 sectors = 32 MiB). The entries are contiguous after userdata. At preflight time, `unixnu_data` probed as exFAT label `UNIXNU`; neither new partition was mounted. The unprivileged Android shell could not read the logs partition contents.

Added source patch `xnu/patches/0143-dali-mt6991-ufs-root-and-logs.patch`, Dali ADT root/log/AO-register properties in `tools/mkadt.py`, the `unixnu_logs` pull path in `tools/sweet-log-pull.sh`, and updated Dali UFS instructions. The XNU path checks MT6991 AO gate/ref-clock state, MTK IP version, UFSHCI version `0x400` / CAP `0x55c7051f`, records host and READ CAPACITY state, rejects outstanding transfer/task requests, and preserves the already-running Android LK host/link instead of replaying HackIOS's UEFI reset/PHY startup. It selects `unixnu_data`, leaves its BlockIo root read-only even if `ufsrw=1` is passed, and targets console writes only at a unique, non-overlapping `unixnu_logs` GPT range. It writes only if the first 4 MiB + header are blank or carry a valid SWEETLOG header; otherwise it refuses. The pull helper reads `unixnu_logs` by default, or legacy `userdata` tail with `-p userdata`.

Committed locally as “Adapt Dali UFS root and persistent logs.” At that point no build, test, flash, partition write, or XNU UFS hardware read had been performed. The source path remains unverified on the handset; see the current handoff above for the later HFSX root write and readback.

At the time of this handoff, the source set had not been built or hardware-tested. `unixnu_data` then needed an HFSX image, and the user had not yet confirmed its write. See the current handoff above: the image has since been written and readback-verified; `unixnu_logs` raw contents remain unknown. A boot trial that may write logs still requires explicit approval.

The patch0142 trial has ended and the stock pair is back on the phone. Patch0125 now enables the saved U3 IPPC port in host mode before U2 for the integrated engine, logs U3 MAC reset status, and restores the exact U3 value after the controller stops. Photo `IMG_20261022_021912.jpg` shows `DALI XHCI: wait timeout stage=attachment reg=430 mask=1 want=1 read=0`, `no USB2 attachment`, then cleanup. In source order, `stage=attachment` is reached only after the No-op command completed successfully; the controller ran and the corrected local clock let the connection wait expire at ~2144ms. This is the first hardware-confirmed xHCI command/DMA-event completion. USB2 PORTSC connection bit stayed clear on selected port2; no enumeration, BOT, capacity/sector read, or root mount.

Patch0123 now waits for PORTSC.PP readback before attachment polling, logs post-power PP/CCS/PLS, and snapshots PORTSC/controller/event state when attachment times out. This follows Intel xHCI 1.22: PP is logical port power, PP=0 suppresses attach reporting, PP must be read back after changes, and USB2 PLS is undefined when PP=0. PP does not prove physical VBUS. Exact patch replay and local ARM64 XHCI compile pass; full CI/package pending. No active trial; original full Android partition hashes verified. ## DC ZVA panic investigation and fix

Photo IMG_20261022_011333.jpg: reset complete pages1/scratch0 then Unaligned kernel data abort, PCfffffe0007b4acc4/LRfffffe0007a892dc/ESR96000061/FARfffffe50422a0040. Exact CI39639794623 disassembly maps PC to `_bzero` DC ZVA x3; LR is DaliXHCI::allocate after bzero(bytes,0x40000). Full PA now preserved (physical2055d0000). Cache-inhibited mapping uses VM_WIMG_IO/Device-GRE for DRAM, where DC ZVA faults even at aligned address. Not an incorrectly aligned arena and not evidence of USB DMA execution. Restore both PDNs, wait100ms for IP sleep, then release own SPM bits; if sleep/ACK fails retain resources and log reboot required. No port, PHY, VUSB rail, host xHCI run, DMA or IRQ writes. Fixed it in commit `c3e0362`; regenerated Dali ADT now reports `interrupt-controller = 'master'` with the same hardware register ranges. Reworked the hunk with stable context and verified it applies to that CI tree. Fix committed/pushed as `79e9408`; workflow guard `e12ef32` now fails future jobs if the macro is absent. CI run `39482079566` succeeded, and the extracted source confirms `DEBUG_BSDINIT 1` under the DEVELOPMENT/SWEET condition.

The user's latest photo shows `kernel_startup_initialize_upto[7, rank -1]` callback trace lines; the user clarified the rank is `-1`. In the matching XNU source, `7` is `STARTUP_SUB_ZALLOC`; `startup_rank_t` is `uint32_t` and `STARTUP_RANK_LAST` is `0xffffffff`, which this `%d` diagnostic renders as `-1`. The printed callback address `0xfffffe0007ab5cd4` resolves in the matching kernel binary to `___zone_site_register`; the changing argument is the per-site registration descriptor. The user confirms the log stopped at this screen and the phone then restarted; the user subsequently entered fastboot. The line is printed before the callback, so this locates the last visible progress within ZALLOC but does not prove which instruction failed or that the callback itself crashed. No panic text or later `reached phase`/`bsd_init` checkpoint is visible; full XNU/userspace boot remains unconfirmed.

No userdata, super, or GPT writes.
- In this payload, `zone_tagging_on` defaults to false and the kernel command line has no `-zt`, so `__zone_site_register()` should return without calling `vm_tag_alloc()`. The visible stop is therefore not enough to attribute the restart to zone tagging; exact cause and time to restart were not captured.

- **Latest clarification:** the phone automatically restarted, then the user manually entered fastboot; elapsed time is unknown. Current Android bootreason/reboot properties refer to the recovery boot and do not establish the XNU reset cause.
- **Diagnostic overhead hypothesis:** ZALLOC is phase 7, earlier than the prior trial's EARLY_BOOT/pthread marker (16). The kernel has 746 __zone_site_register startup entries; synchronous DSI refresh for each long line may significantly slow boot. This has not proven watchdog involvement. Patch application and local logging-behavior validation pass: the actual modified function executes 749 simulated callbacks in the same order, producing 3 summary lines with startup_debug=1 versus 752 with startup_debug=3. Non-SWEET and non-DEVELOPMENT detailed behavior is preserved. Repository validation passes (86 patches). Full kernel build pending; no handset writes this turn.

Internal boot plus external root is a future option requiring new drivers; it does not solve the early ZALLOC restart. Feasibility inquiry only; no USB drive was selected or written.

## Current handoff: dynamic Dali display mapping patch (2026-10-21)

  vbmeta descriptor. This is distinct from the Android boot-v4
  `boot_signature` field, which is zero in both stock and test images. A test

  `avb_ret = 0`; LK proceeded to the logo. The image still did not reach a
  confirmed XNU entry.
- The first footer trial used nonzero boot-v4 OS version/patch fields. The Dali
  boot build now leaves those fields zero, matching the stock GKI v4 header and
  AOSP's boot-v4 requirement. A second footer trial with the corrected header
  still showed only the handset logo. The original `boot_b` was restored after
  each trial and its on-device SHA-256 matched
  `6dc40de74fa7de4f435c513047c51fe10634c1891666031039c52a08b27a3cd5`.

  logs; `/sys/fs/pstore` was empty. `expdb` contains `invalid boot image header`
  lines around the trials, but they also appear in older boot records, so they
  still need correlation with the boot/init_boot/vendor_boot handoff.
- Read-only pulls of the stock images confirmed the actual GKI v4 chain:
  `boot_b` is kernel-only, `init_boot_b` contains the generic LZ4 ramdisk, and
  `vendor_boot_b` contains the DTB plus LZ4 vendor fragments. AOSP requires
  the generic ramdisk after vendor ramdisks in the concatenated initrd. This
  makes the earlier custom payload in `boot`'s ramdisk a plausible cause of
  the logo-only result, though it does not prove it was the only cause.
- `shim/build.sh` now emits a kernel-only Dali boot-v4 image. New
  `shim/build-dali-initboot.sh` and `tools/mkinitboot.py` package a `sweet.payload`
  CPIO entry in a 64 KiB independent-block LZ4 frame and add the stock-style
  AVB v1 hash footer (`Algorithm: NONE`). The local image fills the 8 MiB
  init_boot partition; `unpack_bootimg`, `avbtool info_image`, and LZ4/CPIO
  extraction all validate it. Neither corrected image was flashed.
- `shim/loader.c` now strips an optional Android bootconfig trailer, locates
  the final LZ4 frame in the concatenated initrd, extracts the CPIO payload,
  and streams its segments and ADT to their final
  physical addresses. A temporary host harness decoded the 16,608,008-byte
  ramdisk stream and verified all 10 XNU segment copies plus the 6,892-byte
  ADT against the source payload.
- The Dali boot probe and full shim both build as Android v4 images with zero
  ramdisk size. `xnu/patches/0082-dali-dynamic-framebuffer.patch` makes XNU's
  early diagnostics use `boot_args.Video`; it applies cleanly to the latest
  successful build tree and the four affected C/assembly units pass local
  syntax checks. GitHub Actions run `39425210357` built the patched

  `5a5dbfb4c72fc71f49b24ec92de6005cb407fcfdd7acdc097b4147c25cce2a42`).

- The first paired test used an uncompressed Linux `Image` in `boot_b`. MTK
  `expdb` recorded `No magic number of compression is found!` after LK began

  `08794a432befcf0504ee4933a51260cb6ccc4785b69f345c4b5968e8e6053d2d`.

  fragments and the custom 7,467,394-byte `init_boot` frame. A host harness
  running the same `loader.c` returned `payload verified` and performed the

  matches SHA-256 `6dc40de74fa7de4f435c513047c51fe10634c1891666031039c52a08b27a3cd5`
  and `08794a432befcf0504ee4933a51260cb6ccc4785b69f345c4b5968e8e6053d2d`.
  Android booted normally; `userdata`, `super`, and GPT were not written.
- Diagnostic correction (2026-10-21): automatic shutdown at about 40/50 seconds
  is observed, but the previous interpretation as a specific returned loader
  error is unproven. `exc_handler()` also used a 40-second delay and the old
  code did not subtract console/loader runtime. Do not use those observations
  to rule out exceptions or physical-memory access problems.
- The corrected parse-only probe has absolute deadlines measured from
  `shim_main()` entry. Error stage 0..7 uses 10..80 seconds; complete payload
  parsing uses 90 seconds; exceptions use 110 seconds. It also selects EL1/EL2
  exception registers according to `CurrentEL`. In QEMU, the exact ARM64 image
  returns `payload verified`, stage 8, without an exception. An injected BRK
  reaches the separate 110-second exception path. Hardware diagnosis is next.
- Live Linux maps the device's UFS disk as `target0:0:0:2`; its `userdata`
  block device is on UFS LUN 2. The current SWEET XNU UFS driver hard-codes LUN
  0. `tools/mkadt.py` now emits `lun=2` for Dali (and `lun=0` for Sweet), and
  patch `0028` reads that ADT property. The remote XNU build includes the
  patch, but UFS takeover and a full-root boot remain untested.
- The D22AP `/product` node has now been extracted from the iPhone X IPSW and
  is included in the generated Dali ADT.
- Live facts used so far: candidate XNU RAM `0x200000000 + 0x13fe00000`, GICD

**Phone workflow (user does buttons, Claude runs commands):**
- Boot test kernel: phone in fastboot -> `fastboot boot shim/out/shim-boot-X.img`. NEVER `fastboot getvar`.
- **Read logs WITHOUT filming:** after a test the user holds Power+VolDown -> fastboot -> `fastboot boot recovery/recovery.img` (OrangeFox, temp) -> wait for `adb shell true` -> `tools/sweet-log-pull.sh /run/media/dipa/android/ios-sweet-work/logs/<name>.txt` (reads the 4 MiB console ring the kernel keeps in the last 1025 blocks of userdata; patch 0047). Then `adb reboot bootloader` brings the phone back to fastboot without the user.
- **Flash a root image:** `fastboot flash -S 512M userdata <img>.simg` (7 min, 25 chunks). After a big flash the Xiaomi ABL fastboot hangs: next `fastboot boot` stalls at "Sending" -> the USER must re-enter fastboot by hand (Power+VolDown). Do not retry commands against the hung ABL. A flash right after `adb reboot bootloader` once failed on chunk 1 (USB protocol error): just retry. User must NOT unplug during flash (once interrupted at 25/25 -> reflash).
- **Edit the phone root without reflashing (same-length only):** find the file's raw offset in the local image (it is one extent; search the image for its first 4 KiB), compare with `adb exec-out dd if=/dev/block/sda34 ...`, then `adb push` changed 4 KiB blocks and `dd of=/dev/block/sda34 seek=... conv=notrunc,fsync`, read back. Recovery kernel has no hfsplus.
- **Pull the whole phone root** (12 min): `adb exec-out "dd if=/dev/block/sda34 bs=1048576 count=16384" > img`; mount ro with udisksctl or `sudo mount -t hfsplus -o loop,ro`. To write into a pulled image: `sudo fsck.hfsplus -fy` first (volume is never cleanly unmounted -> Linux mounts ro even with -o force), then `sudo mount -t hfsplus -o loop,force`.
- Making images: copy latest img with `cp --sparse=always`, mount rw with force, change, `sync; umount`, `sha256sum`, `img2simg`. Keep only the latest image + one fallback on the SSD (/home is ~98% full; big work files live in `/run/media/dipa/android/ios-sweet-work/`, old kernels/images archived in `.../ios-sweet-work/archive/`).

**Diagnostics built into the kernel (all SWEET-only):** persistent console log (0047, boot-arg sweetlog=0 off; flush every ~1 s); `|name| text` copies of /dev/null writes for processes in `sweetnull` (default backboardd,SpringBoard; 0048); `IOK name:` IOKit lookup/open trace for `sweetiolog` (default backboardd,SpringBoard; 0049/0054, 400 lines max); `SWEET Sandbox: proc op N` (0050); `STR ...` heap text dump on SIGABRT/SIGTRAP for `sweetscan` (default backboardd,SpringBoard; 0051/0053) - this is how NSException/BSAssert reasons were found; `SMP name tN pc .. bt ..` user stack sampler for `sweetsample` (default SpringBoard,backboardd; first at 90 s then every 120 s, 40 samples, 16 frames; 0054/0055); `SWEET FB: N swaps` every 1000 swaps; `CRASH name ...` crash reports (0043/0044). Symbolicate cache addresses with `ipsw dyld a2s research/dsc/dyld_shared_cache_arm64 <addr - slide>`; main-binary addresses: addr - mh + 0x100000000.

- **v11 still migrated (boot 0056u, 20:32 UTC, `logs/boot-0056u-v11.txt`).** On iOS kCFPreferencesCurrentHost lands in the plain plist: all DMEnvironment setters (setLastBuildVersionPref, setUserDataDispositionPref, ...) use CurrentHost and the phone's `/var/mobile/Library/Preferences/com.apple.migration.plist` holds DMUserDataDisposition; datamigrator's XPC service runs with `_RoleAccount: mobile`. **Image v12** = v11 + `LastSystemVersion` = 20H392 added to that plain `com.apple.migration.plist` (keeps DMUserDataDisposition, 501:501 0600) + `/Library/Audio/Plug-Ins/HAL/VirtualAudio.plugin` moved to `/Library/Audio/Plug-Ins/HAL.disabled/` (Codex: stops the mediaserverd SIGSEGV loop; untested). **installcoordinationd loop explained (Codex `codex/installcoordinationd-report.md`):** os_crash "Failed to create data directory" EACCES for `/private/var/containers/Shared/SystemGroup/systemgroup.com.apple.installcoordinationd/Library/InstallCoordination`: `Library`/`Library/Caches` in the container are ACL-only (uid/gid 0xfffffffe, mode 0000, ACE for group GUID ...012B = gid 299 systemusers); with `config_ext_resolver` the kernel asks an identity resolver (memberd/opendirectoryd) that iOS does not run -> EWOULDBLOCK -> ACE skipped -> EACCES. **Patch 0057**: drop config_ext_resolver from FILESYS_BASE (compatibility UID/GID<->GUID mapping is used instead), likely fixes other ACL-protected containers too. Codex task 3: UFS driver performance design (`codex/ufs-performance.md`).
- **mediaserverd SIGSEGV loop explained (Codex report `/run/media/dipa/android/ios-sweet-work/codex/mediaserverd-report.md`):** the fault (FAR 0x390) is in the HAL plug-in `/Library/Audio/Plug-Ins/HAL/VirtualAudio.plugin/VirtualAudio` (ApplyBeamFormerPreference <- VirtualAudio_PlugIn::SetPropertyData <- HALS_AHPPlugIn::ObjectSetPropertyData <- AudioObjectSetPropertyData <- MediaExperience vaemVADSerializationListener): the default virtual audio device ('vdef', VirtualAudioDevice_Default) does not exist (no audio hardware/driver, no /product/audio). Proposed workaround for GUI bring-up: move VirtualAudio.plugin out of /Library/Audio/Plug-Ins/HAL/ in the next root image (untested). v11 reflashed OK after an interrupted flash (USB unplugged at chunk 25/25). Codex task 2: installcoordinationd crash loop.
- **ROOT CAUSE of the endless migration: no IOPlatformUUID (2026-10-17).** datamigrator's `-[DMMigrator isMigrationNeededWithBuildVersion:lastBuildVersion:clientPID:]` gets lastBuildVersion from `-[DMEnvironment lastBuildVersionPref]` = `CFPreferencesCopyValue("LastSystemVersion", "com.apple.migration", kCFPreferencesCurrentUser, kCFPreferencesCurrentHost)` -> a ByHost file. The phone had `/var/mobile/Library/Preferences/com.apple.migration.plist` (only DMUserDataDisposition) and NO `Preferences/ByHost` directory at all. On arm XNU publishes IOPlatformUUID (gethostuuid) only from `/chosen/unique-chip-id` (8 bytes) + `/chosen/chip-id` (4 bytes) (IOPlatformExpertDevice::generatePlatformUUID, called from IOPlatformExpert::start); the SWEET ADT had neither, so every CurrentHost preference write failed. Fix: mkadt adds `unique-chip-id` = 0x5357454554000001 (synthetic) and `chip-id` = 0x8015 -> UUID `A9683613-3704-53F2-95D0-98A8FB574E78` (same SHA1 namespace computation as the kernel). ADT `shim/out/sweet-0057hi.adt`, boot image `shim/out/shim-boot-0056u.img` (0056 kernel + 0057hi ADT, QEMU OK). **Image v11** = v10 + `Preferences/ByHost/com.apple.migration.<UUID upper and lower>.plist` {LastSystemVersion: 20H392} (501:501, dir 0755 files 0600). - **IOSurface values ABI (offline RE, `/run/media/dipa/android/ios-sweet-work/iosurface-values.md`) - TODO in SweetSurface (0046 only accepts and drops values, so IOSurfaceCopyValue always returns NULL):** sel 9 SetValue in = {u32 id, u32 pad, IOCFSerialize([value, key]) or ([dict] = merge)}, sel 0xb RemoveValue in = {u32 id, u32 pad, key\0} (empty key = remove all); both out 4 bytes = new generation, client accepts only (gen+2)|1. Kernel after each change: gen = (gen+2)|1 (odd, never 0) -> state+0x20, serialized size (>=1) -> state+0x28. sel 0xa in {u32 id, u32 0, u8 0}, out {u32 ?, u32 gen, serialized non-bulk dict}; too small -> 0xe00002db + needed size (client retries x16). sel 0x1b in 0x88: bulk struct 0x70 @0, u64 valid size @0x70, u64 changed mask @0x78, u32 id @0x80 -> copy masked fields into state+0x30 (bit0 32B @0, bit1 16B @0x20, bit2 8B @0x30, bits3-10 1B @0x38..0x3f, bits11-14 24B @0x40/4B @0x58/8B @0x5c/1B @0x64).
- **IOMFB display matching confirmed (offline RE, `/run/media/dipa/android/ios-sweet-work/iomfb-match.md`):** the framework never matches on "AppleCLCD"; any registered IOMobileFramebuffer subclass works. `iomfb_match_callback` reads only `dfr`/`AppleTV`/`external` (CFBoolean, service itself, no parents); internal main display = all absent/false (type 0, name "primary"). Display count = /arm-io children whose device_type starts with "display-subsystem" (count 0 -> CreateDisplayList returns NULL; more nodes than services -> 30 s wait). IOMobileFramebufferOpen fails only on IOServiceOpen failure; canvas-width/height read as 4-byte CFData from the IOService-plane parent (optional). CopyProperty = IORegistryEntrySearchCFProperty on the service only. Patch 0046 (disp0 node, `external`=false, AppleCLCD : IOMobileFramebuffer) fits all of this.
- **IOMobileFramebuffer swap + notification ABI (2026-10-17, offline).** Swap record = fb+0x18, **0x46c bytes**, zeroed by SwapBegin and sent whole by `kern_SwapEnd` = IOConnectCallStructMethod(sel 5, rec, 0x46c, no output). **SwapBegin** = sel 4, no input, 1 scalar out = swap id (stored at rec+0x50). `kern_SwapSetLayer(fb, layer<4, IOSurfaceRef, CGRect a, CGRect b, flags)`: rec+0x54+4*layer = **IOSurface id**; rec+0x104 bit layer = layer enabled (rec+0x108 bit cleared); rect a (int x,y,w,h, scaled) at rec+0x64+16*layer, rect b at rec+0xc4+16*layer; rec+0x114+4*layer = transform from flags&0xf; flag bits 5/31/6/7/8/9 set bytes rec+0x2ca/0x2cc/0x2e3/0x2e5/0x2e6/0x467+layer. A NULL surface only sets the enable bit with zero id. SwapWait = sel 6 scalars [swap id, timeout, 0].
  - **Notifications**: `IOMobileFramebufferScheduleWithDispatchQueue`/`GetRunLoopSource` create an IONotificationPort and `IOConnectSetNotificationPort(conn, type, port, 0)` (kernel `registerNotificationPort`). `kern_EnableNotifications(fb, type, cb, ctx)` = **sel 72 scalars [NotifyCallbackFuncs[type], fb, type, 0]** -> the kernel keeps an async reference {port, func, refcon=fb} per type and fires it with `sendAsyncResult64`. Types: 0 HotPlug, 1 Power, 3 FrameInfo (args[0] = mask of 31-bit keys, args[1] u32, then values), 4 CRC, **5 Vsync**: 6 args, each a 32-bit half in a u64 slot: (ts lo, ts hi), (t2 lo, hi) passed through the time-ingest helper, (t3 lo, hi); the framework then calls the client's vsync callback(fb, ts, t2', t3, ctx). Vsync callbacks are dropped while the notification port has queued messages.

- **IOSurface.framework user-client ABI reversed (2026-10-17, offline, step 2 of the GUI plan).** `ipsw dyld disass research/dsc/dyld_shared_cache_arm64 --image IOSurface` (selector scan script: scratchpad `selscan.py`).
  - Connect (`_iosConnectInitalize`, pthread_once): `IOServiceGetMatchingService(IOServiceNameMatching("IOCoreSurfaceRoot"))`, retried with sleep(1) up to 241 times (a missing service stalls every client ~4 min, no crash); `IOServiceOpen(type 0)`; then **sel 13** (no inputs, struct out 0x18 bytes = `_ioSurfaceLimits`) and requires u16 at out+0x16 == 1 (ABI version); out+0 = offset/plane-base alignment mask, out+4 = bytes-per-row alignment mask (used as (x+m)&~m), +0..+0x14 also copied into IOSurface remote-method init, otherwise logs and closes the connection.
  - **Create = sel 0**: scalar in [parent id or 0], struct in = `IOCFSerialize(props, kIOCFSerializeToBinary)` (OSUnserializeBinary format; keys IOSurfaceWidth/Height/BytesPerRow/BytesPerElement/PixelFormat/AllocSize/PlaneInfo/CacheMode/IsGlobal/...), struct out **0xa68 bytes** ("lock result"). **Lookup = sel 4**: scalar in [id], same struct out. LookupFromMachPort = sel 0x22 (scalar in [port name]); CreateMachPort = sel 0x23 (scalar in [id, options], scalar out [port name]).
  - Lock result (copied to client+0x68, size 0x68 + planes*0x80, min 0xe8): 0x00 u64 base address **mapped in the caller's task**; 0x08 u64 task VA of a per-surface shared state page (+0 u64 registry id, +8/+0xa u16 bound accel ids, +0xc u32 seed, +0x14 u8 tiled, +0x20 u32 value generation, +0x24 YCbCr matrix, +0x28 u32 non-bulk values size, +0x2d u8 displayable, +0x2e u8 sysmem-only, +0x30 0x70 bytes bulk attachments); 0x10 u64 task VA of timestamp page (4 u64 indexed timestamps, +0x20 detach code); 0x18 u32 surface id; 0x1c u32 alloc size; 0x20 width; 0x24 height; 0x28 bytes per row; 0x2c buffer offset; 0x30 pixel format; 0x34 u32 (dcache flush length); 0x38 plane count; 0x40 u16 bytes/element; 0x42 u8 element width; 0x43 u8 element height; 0x44 cache mode; 0x4c u8 subsampling; 0x4d u8 prohibit-use count; 0x50 parent id; 0x54 slice count; 0x58 u64 trace id; 0x60 u64 protection options; planes[n] at 0x68+n*0x80 (+0 width, +4 height, +8 bytes/row, +0xc offset, +0x14 size, +0x18 u16 bpe, +0x1a/+0x1b u8 element w/h, +0x1c u8 component count, +0x1d address format, +0x54 u8 compression type (non-zero -> no CPU base address)).
  - **Traps** (IOConnectTrapN -> `IOUserClient::getTargetAndTrapForIndex`): 0 IncrementUseCount(id, category), 1 DecrementUseCount(id, category), 2 **Lock**(id, options, &seed_out), 3 **Unlock**(id, options, &seed_out), 4 Release(id), 5 Release after a failed create/lookup setup (id).
  - Other selectors: 5 SetYCbCrMatrix, 6 WrapClientImage, 7 WrapClientMemory, 9 SetValue (serialized), 0xa UpdateNonBulkValueCache (struct in 9), 0xb RemoveValue, 0xc BindAccel, 0x11 SetSurfaceNotify (async), 0x12 RemoveSurfaceNotify, 0x13 Log, 0x14 SetPurgeable, 0x15 SetOwnership, 0x16 SetTiled, 0x18 SetTimestamp, 0x19 GetTileFormat, 0x1a CopyDataValueBytes, 0x1b SetBulkAttachments (struct 0x88), 0x1e/0x2e-0x31 (via outlined helpers), 0x1f SetCompressedTileDataRegionMemoryUsed, 0x20 GetGraphicsCommPageAddress, 0x21 SetIndexedTimestamp, 0x24-0x26/0x2d IOSurfaceAccelerator, 0x27-0x29 shared events, 0x2a SignalEvent, 0x2b Set/InitDetachModeCode, 0x2c SetOwnershipIdentity, 0x32-0x34 data properties.
  - => SWEET kernel service: class/name **IOCoreSurfaceRoot** + IOSurfaceRootUserClient; minimum = sel 13, 0, 4, 9/0xa/0xb (values), traps 0-5, 0x22/0x23 later for cross-process surfaces (CA sends surfaces to backboardd by mach port). Memory: IOBufferMemoryDescriptor per surface, mapped into each task with createMappingInTask; ids from a global table.

- **Image v8: daemon logs on the console.** `tools/patch-launchd-plist.py IN OUT LABEL...` rewrites launchd.plist (binary, plistlib): renames every `_PanicOnCrash` to `_PanicOnCrasX` and gives the listed jobs `StandardOutPath`/`StandardErrorPath` `/dev/console` (devfs console is 0622, writable by mobile) plus `OS_ACTIVITY_DT_MODE=YES` (os_log mirrored to stderr) and `CFLOG_FORCE_STDERR=1`. v8 = v7 + that plist for com.apple.backboardd and com.apple.SpringBoard, written through the mount (796310 bytes, root:80 0644), SHA256 in `ios16-root-20H392-v8.img.sha256`, `.simg` ready; boot with `shim/out/shim-boot-0045n.img`. Never patch file contents through raw offsets again; write through the mount.

- **HIGHMEM: XNU in the upper DDR bank, ~3.95 GiB (was 1.2 GiB).** Live FDT memory = <0x80000000 2G> + <0x1_00000000 0xfcc00000>; all fixed reserved-memory carve-outs are in the low bank (highest: ramdump_fb 0xaf000000, rmtfs ~0xfde01000), dynamic pools have alloc-ranges <= 0xffffffff, UFS node has no `iommus` (Linux DMAs anywhere). `tools/mkadt.py --highmem` sets /chosen dram-base 0x100000000 dram-size 0xfcc00000; `tools/mkpayload.py` now takes phys_base/mem_size from the ADT (default payloads byte-identical, verified); `tools/qemu-sweet.py` sizes -m from the payload (8G for highmem). Patch **0034** (`0034-sweet-ufs-dma-above-4g.patch`): UFS refuses DMA memory above 4G without UFSHCI CAP.64AS (bit 24), logs `DMA at 0x...`. CI `38905889883` success -> `xnu/out-0034`. **QEMU verified** (`research/q0034-hi`, ADT `research/q0034-hi.adt` --qemu --highmem, HFS RAM disk): phys_base 0x100000000, init PID 1 heartbeat, no panic; `max_mem` = `mem_size` = 0xfcc00000 read via QMP (`research/q0034-hi-mem`). Phone images: `shim/out/shim-boot-0034hr.img` (read-only) / `-0034hw.img` (+ufsrw=1 rootrw=1), ADT `shim/out/sweet-0034hi.adt` (asmb SIP + sweet-ufs + highmem). PHONE RISK: unknown runtime TZ/XPU protection in the upper bank would show as an instant reset; fall back to 0033r/w. Watchdog ruled out for the overnight reset: msm-watchdog bark is 20 s (qcom,bark-time 0x4e20) yet panic screens stay up for many minutes.

- Faulting instruction: `ldr x0, [x8, #0x150]` in crypto_random_kmem_ctx_size; x8 = NULL g_crypto_funcs. Source: libkern/crypto/corecrypto_rand.c and osfmk/vm/vm_kern.c:2448. rand.h explicitly permits NON-cryptographic kmem RNGs, but requires no locking in generation and context size <=256. Do not substitute read_random here: the global PRNG provider is also unregistered and kmem generation must remain lock-free. early_random may seed contexts during single-CPU bootstrap; it uses the built-in HMAC-SHA256 DRBG with the host-generated ADT seed.
- Patch 0013 keeps the replacement entirely private to vm_kern.c: SplitMix64 state is 8 bytes per CPU/sizeclass, initialized using early_random. Uniform selection uses rejection sampling to avoid modulo bias. Other platforms retain the original corecrypto calls; SWEET's generic crypto APIs/registration remain untouched. Reference: https://prng.di.unimi.it/splitmix64.c (Sebastiano Vigna, public domain).

## QEMU emulation (2026-10-15)
- `tools/mkadt.py OUT.adt --qemu` = same ADT with QEMU virt GICD 0x08000000 / GICR 0x080a0000 / CNTFRQ 62.5 MHz.
- `tools/qemu-sweet.py PAYLOAD OUTDIR [--secs N] [--hmp "x /8gx ADDR"] [--gdb]`: host-side copy of shim/loader.c
  (segments + ADT + boot_args at their PAs, 6-instruction stub sets x0 and jumps), `-M virt,gic-version=3 -cpu cortex-a76`
  (same ID_AA64MMFR0 as Kryo 470), 4G RAM so 0x9c000000 fb and 0xb0000000 kernel are plain RAM.
  Outputs `fb.png` (the screen), `regs.txt`, `qemu.log` (exceptions with ESR/FAR/ELR). `--gdb` halts with a stub on :1234
  (system `gdb` + `set architecture aarch64`, `hbreak *VA` works with the MMU on).
- Not emulated: Qualcomm XPUs, ABL, real panel, PSCI conduit (QEMU virt uses hvc). Hardware still decides those.
- Example: `python3 tools/mkadt.py /tmp/q.adt --qemu && python3 tools/mkpayload.py xnu/out-0013/kernel.development.sweet /tmp/q.adt /tmp/pq.bin --cmdline 'debug=0xc serial=3 cpus=1 keepsyms=1 -enable_kprintf_spam' && python3 tools/qemu-sweet.py /tmp/pq.bin /tmp/q`

## Layout
- `backup/`  partition dumps + full sdb..sdf LUNs, `SHA256SUMS` (all verified on-device == local)
- `dtb/`     live FDT (from OrangeFox), PixelOS boot.img dtb, dtbo, dts versions
- `shim/`    bare-metal boot shim (`./build.sh` -> `out/shim-boot.img`)
- `research/` mainline sm7150 dts, XNU pe_fiq.c etc, PocketDarwin patches
- `tools/unpack_bootv2.py`

## Device access
- No root in PixelOS 17 (user build, adb root disabled). Root = `fastboot boot recovery/recovery.img` (OrangeFox R12.0_2, temp boot, not flashed).
- `fastboot boot` works for stock boot.img, OrangeFox, and our shim.
- Shim resets itself via PSCI SYSTEM_RESET; IMEM restart_reason 0x77665500 is seen
  (bootreason=bootloader) but ABL still boots Android, so it does not stop in fastboot.

## Hardware facts (live DT + shim measurements)
- Framebuffer (ABL cont splash): 0x9c000000, 1080x2400, stride 4320, a8r8g8b8, panel is DSC *video* mode (no kick needed).
- RAM: 0x80000000+2G, 0x100000000+0xfcc00000. Live reserved-memory = PixelOS base dtb + sweet dtbo fixups.
- GICv3: GICD 0x17a00000, GICR 0x17a60000 (stride 0x20000, 8 PEs). GICD_TYPER 0x007B041D: SecurityExtn=1 (DS=0).
  ABL leaves GICD_CTLR = 0x10 (ARE_NS on, groups off). Writing 0x13 reads back 0x12: EnableGrp1A (bit1) is the one that matters.
- GIC Group 1 VERIFIED (shim 2c): vtimer INTID 19 = 30 IRQs/300ms, ptimer INTID 18 = 30, SGI 1 to self = 10/10.
  ICC_SRE=1 already, ICC_CTLR 0x8440, priority/enable writes stick. GICR_IGROUPR0 and GICD_IGROUPRn read 0 from NS
  even though Group 1 delivery works (RAZ in NS view) - do not use them as a health check.
  INTID 28 shows pending constantly (unknown PPI 12).
- Timer 19.2 MHz. Qualcomm PPI numbering: secure-phys/phys/virt/hyp = PPI 1/2/3/0 -> vtimer INTID 19, ptimer 18 (NOT 27/30).
- PSCI 1.1 via SMC, SMCCC 1.1. CPU_ON ok; SYSTEM_SUSPEND and SYSTEM_RESET2 unsupported.
- SMP: CPU_ON works for all 7 secondaries (MPIDR 0x81000n00).
- Entry EL1, MMU off, x0 = DTB (magic ok). ID_AA64MMFR0 = ...101122: 16K granule YES, 4K YES.
- No serial console: debug UART SE is in SPI mode on sweet. Screen is the only debug channel.

## XNU implications
- XNU GICv3 path (pexpert/arm/pe_fiq.c, VMAPPLE) puts the timer (PPI 27) in Group 0 = FIQ. With SecurityExtn=1,
  Group 0 is secure -> impossible from NS EL1. Need: Group 1 IRQ, ICC_IGRPEN1, INTID 19, and the timer handled in the IRQ path.
- XNU VMAPPLE PSCI uses `hvc`; sweet needs `smc`.
- PocketDarwin NonAppleGenericCpu.patch only swaps -mcpu=apple-h7 for -march=armv8-a.

## Milestone 3 (2026-10-14): 16K MMU verified
- ABL loads the Image at 0x80080000 (base 0x80000000 + 0x80000) regardless of text_offset=0.
- 16K granule, T0SZ=28 (L2 start, 32MB blocks), MAIR 0x44ff00, TCR 0x1409CB51C, PARange=2 (40-bit PA).
- Only own image pages WB (Qualcomm XPUs: never map carve-outs cacheable); fb Normal-NC; rest Device.
- AT S1E1R checks all as expected; unmapped -> translation fault L2.
- Fill fb: 110 ms (MMU off) -> 3.5 ms (NC). Memory loop 70 ms -> 0.7 ms. vtimer + SGI IRQs still fine with MMU on.

## XNU loader design notes (xnu-8796)
- Entry: `_start` -> `start_first_cpu`, x0 = boot_args (PA), MMU off, EL1. boot_args: Revision/Version 2,
  virtBase/physBase/memSize/topOfKernelData, Boot_Video (fb 0x9c000000, rowBytes 4320, 1080x2400, depth 32),
  deviceTreeP/Length (ADT), CommandLine[608], bootFlags, memSizeActual.
- start.s maps [kernel mach header .. physBase+memSize) with cacheable BLOCKS. On Qualcomm every XPU carve-out
  must therefore lie below the kernel: use physBase = 0xb0000000 (above ramdump_fb 0xaf000000+16M,
  cdsp_sec, qseecom), end before rmtfs 0xfde01000 -> memSize ~0x4d000000 for bring-up.
- Base ADT: iPhone 8 d20ap DeviceTree from iOS 16.7.16 IPSW (tools/remotezip.py + tools/adt.py; LZFSE payload).
  Template values are empty: the loader must fill chosen/*, memory-map, cpu*/reg etc. like iBoot.
- XNU build: GitHub Actions (dipa-studio/ios-sweet, xnu.yml) via blacktop/darwin-xnu-build, MACOS_VERSION=13.5.
  New-clang fixes in xnu/patches; Xcode 16 iig is incompatible (IORPC.kernelContent) -> macos-14 runner, oldest Xcode.

## Kernel architecture plan (SWEET machine config)
- First stock build (VMAPPLE) is arm64e (PAC). Kryo 470 = ARMv8.2, no PAC -> SWEET must build plain arm64
  (HAS_APPLE_PAC is only defined for __arm64e__, so arm64 drops PAC automatically).
- Template: VMAPPLE (16K pages, GICv3 code in pe_fiq.c) minus APPLEVIRTUALPLATFORM hypercalls/paravirt PAC,
  plus BCM2837 (generic ARM: no APPLE_ARM64_ARCH_FAMILY Apple sysregs). Do not link the KDK lib<cfg>.os.*.a.
- Timer: XNU expects it on FIQ (sleh_fiq -> rtclock_intr). SWEET: GIC Group 1, so sleh_irq must ack ICC_IAR1,
  route INTID 19 to the same timer path, SGIs to cpu_signal_handler, EOI via ICC_EOIR1.
- SMP/interrupts: open-source AppleARMSMP (USE_APPLEARMSMP) needs an IOInterruptController (AIC role) and an
  IOPMGR (CPU power -> PSCI CPU_ON via SMC). Plan: built-in IOKit classes (SweetGIC, SweetPMGR, platform
  expert) compiled into the kernel and matched via iokit/KernelConfigTables.cpp -> no closed kexts needed.
- Debug: xnu patch 0003 sweet-fbcon = kprintf drawn on the framebuffer (ADT node "sweet-fbcon").
- ADT /arm-io ranges must not map to parent 0 (pe_arm_get_soc_base_phys()==0 disables serial): use parent 0x1000000.

## Current: MTU3 v2 default force bits identified; host probe restored (2026-10-21)

CI run 39566932800 / commit a0d6f0b passed build, init diagnostics, and artifact stages. The paired image was flashed only after fresh Android/root/slot/hash preflight. Photos IMG_20261021_162316.jpg and IMG_20261021_162320.jpg show source VBUS 4.977–4.989V for the bounded 15s lease; SC8585/TCPC and source cleanup passed. AO domains, bus-protection ACKs, clocks/fences, and USB AO gate were ready. IPPC identity/caps matched, but the guarded candidate saw ctrl0=0x10411021, ctrl1=1, ctrl2=1, sts1/sts2=0 and SPM_CTRL_V2=0x1f00/ACK=0. It conservatively rejected before any IPPC, request-gate, host-PDN, PHY, xHCI, or DMA write; xHCI capability reads and USB enumeration did not occur. Final PDVBUS decayed to 2.173V and CHGVIN=32mV.

Vendor `mtu3_hw_regs.h` identifies bits 8–14 as `SSUSB_SPM_FORCE_HW_REQ_MSK` (0x7f00), and vendor `mtu3_plat.c` says MT6989+ enables these by default then clears them for MTU3 v2 startup. The same source opens the eight REQ_GATE registers at `usbcfg_ao` offsets 0x70–0x8c before the SPM request. Patch 0104 now follows this exact bounded sequence, preserves the separate PMIC bit, restores the saved REQ_GATE and SPM values, and restores host/device PDN, waits for IP sleep with reset released, then restores saved ctrl0 before releasing resources. The ADT white-list now includes the exact `usbcfg_ao` range 0x16790000/0x100. No port selection, PHY tuning, xHCI run/stop, IRQ, DMA, or enumeration is added. Local ARM64 object compilation passed for DaliUSBHost.cpp and DaliUSBPMIC.cpp; patch dry-run against the CI 0103 tree passed.

IMPORTANT audit correction: Intel xHCI1.22 sec5.4.8(p405, PDF page404) explicitly requires HCHalted=0 before writing PORTSC. Previous PP probes deliberately kept xHCI halted, so their readback/cleanup observations do not certify a standards-compliant host initialization, nor prove missing analog calibration is the sole CCS0 cause. Sec4.19.4 permits connection reporting before Run, but that does NOT waive the PORTSC write requirement. Source: https://www.intel.com/content/dam/www/public/us/en/documents/technical-specifications/extensible-host-controler-interface-usb-xhci.pdf . Do not reuse halted PP diagnostic as the next experiment.

Remaining integrated work: audit DMA physical addressing/IOMMU/cache ownership and driver interfaces; implement initialized halted reset->DCBAA/scratchpads/command+event rings->Run with bounded cleanup, then compliant PORTSC control and port reset/device descriptor enumeration; USB MSC read-only block transport/filesystem/root handoff. ## Current handoff: TWRP MTU3 host parity patch ready for full CI (2026-10-22)

The new upstream `1664d63` panic-region change is diagnostics-only; it does not fix USB attach. The TWRP source audit is more useful for USB: `mtu3_dr.c` defers the Type-C host role while the DT advertises USB offload and no provider is registered. The Dali TWRP module returns `SSUSB_OFFLOAD_MODE_NONE`; registration replays the queued host role, after which the vendor Linux MTU3/xHCI/PHY path enumerates U-disks. This Linux `.ko` is not usable in XNU; XNU owns those registers directly.

Patch0126 mirrors the native host path where the previous XNU code diverged: it waits for U3 MAC reset/SYS125/REF/SYSPLL after U3 host enable; asserts the Dali OTG select bit; applies masked/readback-checked native MAC settings for `force-vbus=false`, Gen1 deemphasis, USB2 L1=0x474, SOF_KEEP, and LDM delay 1. It restores temporary tuning after xHCI stops and keeps forced-VBUS bits clear like MTU3. Exact patch replay and local ARM64 syntax compile both pass. CI workflow now asserts the new source and linked marker.

Phone safety: connected Android verification passed (`dali`, slot `_b`, boot_completed, root); original boot_b/init_boot_b full physical sizes/SHA256 verified by `device_pair.py verify .`. No active trial or pending restore. The direct USB photo still shows `CCS=0`; there has been no USB device enumeration, disk sector read or root mount.

Next: commit/push patch0126 and workflow assertions; run full fork CI; validate actual source and paired image, then prepare the complete candidate for one consolidated OTG/U-disk trial. Do not flash an incomplete candidate.

## 2026-10-22 — USB power indicator points to an early VBUS shutdown

User reports the U-disk power LED flashes around `DALI CP` and then goes dark. The captured sequence shows the charge-pump/source-routing handoff, then TCPC source enable and an early ADC reading near 4.98V before MTU3/xHCI starts. Earlier complete trials end with VBUS near 2.2V after cleanup. In the current XNU source, a safe no-attach scan ends its worker after the ~5.2s wait; `DaliUSBPower` then calls `finishLocked("host worker ended")` and turns the TCPC source off. The 15s lease is a second cutoff. This explains the LED behavior plausibly, but it does not prove that the external VBUS regulator itself failed while the scan ran.

Patch `0128-dali-hold-vbus-through-host-rescans.patch` removes the worker-ended cutoff, retries only scans whose MTU3/PHY/repeater/port/DMA cleanup verified, and holds the 5V/500mA source through a 60s powered window. It logs VBUS while a scan is idle; detach, voltage/fault status, cleanup refusal, and the 60s bound still end ordinary trials. A verified read-only root supply gets its own active-root state so the diagnostic cap only lifts after the disk is supplied. The local ARM64 syntax compile of `DaliUSBHost.cpp` and `DaliUSBPower.cpp` passed (0/2 failures); patch dry-run against the exact patch0127 CI source passed. Workflow assertions cover the source hold, safe retry, root-active state and 60s cap. Full CI and hardware validation remain pending. The handset is on its physically verified original Android boot pair; no new candidate is flashed.

## 2026-10-23 — Offline audit: TCPC attached-source termination and current limit

XNU's Type-C handoff is correct to arm both CC pins as default Rp while waiting for a partner (`ROLE_CTRL=0x05`). After three stable Rd samples, however, it changes only the polarity bit (`0x419`) and enables VBUS; it never changes `ROLE_CTRL`, and the periodic check requires it to remain `0x05` throughout the powered host scan (`expected-DaliUSBPower-0128.cpp:361-400`).

The local MT6379 driver has an attached-source branch in `mt6379_set_cc()`: for `pull == TYPEC_CC_RP` and `typec_state == typec_attached_src`, it sets the unused CC pin to Rd according to polarity before encoding ROLE_CTRL (`audit/tcpc_mt6379.c:1681-1717`). Given the TCPCI field layout (`../Kernel/Xiaomi_Kernel_OpenSource/include/linux/usb/tcpci.h`), that callback would encode CC1-active Rp/CC2 Rd as `0x09`, or CC1 Rd/CC2-active Rp as `0x06` at default Rp; with the live DT's `tcpc,rp-level = <1>` (1.5 A), corresponding values are `0x19`/`0x16`. These values are source-derived expectations, not observed Android register readbacks: the saved attached-host captures report Type-C source/host roles but do not dump TCPC ROLE_CTRL.

There is also a power-policy delta: the live DT advertises Rp level 1 (1.5 A), while XNU arms default Rp and programs a 500 mA current limit. The Android regulator_summary's 1400 mA `current` column is the configured limit returned by get_current_limit(), not measured stick consumption (`drivers/regulator/core.c:6014-6022`). The Android USB framework capture reports configuration max_power=200 for this Kingston stick (`ci-artifacts/android-otg-wireless/usb-service.txt`), so 500 mA exceeds its declared steady-state configuration. A brief startup inrush is unmeasured, but the saved evidence weakens the undersupply hypothesis. Do not raise the limit speculatively.

Treat this as a new, testable CC-role/current discrepancy, not a confirmed explanation for xHCI `CCS=0`. A future diagnostic should record ROLE_CTRL, CC status/orientation, PMIC current/fault, OTG_STS, DP/DM and PORTSC across attach/source-on. Keep this separate from the host-ready-before-VBUS sequencing candidate where possible. No source, device, network, build or test changes were made in this offline audit.


## Offline current-limit evidence correction (2026-10-23)

The Android `regulator_summary` value of 1400 mA is a configured regulator current limit, not the stick's actual current draw: `drivers/regulator/core.c` prints `_regulator_get_current_limit_unlocked()` in that column (lines 6014-6022). Android USB service separately reports the Kingston configuration `max_power=200` (`research/dali-usb-root/ci-artifacts/android-otg-wireless/usb-service.txt`). The XNU source path limits output to 500 mA, which is above that declared steady-state configuration; a transient startup peak remains unmeasured. This makes inadequate steady current less likely, but does not establish the actual inrush.

XNU's current `DaliUSBPower::snapshot()` measures TCPCI VBUS plus PMIC PDVBUS and CHGVIN voltages, and `readADC()` explicitly permits only ADC channels 11 and 1 (`research/dali-usb-root/ci-artifacts/expected-DaliUSBPower-0128.cpp:58-101,132-141`). The live base DT includes an `MT6379_ADC_IBUS` input in the charger channel list (`research/dali-usb-root/audit/mt6991.dts:20536-20545`), but the matching ADC binding/driver source is absent from the local kernel checkout. Its raw scale and whether it measures the OTG output path cannot be verified offline, so no current-sensor change is justified yet. The stronger remaining evidence is still the absent native host `OTG_STS` signature and pre-enumeration USB2 RxDetect state.


## 2026-10-23 — Corrected native/XNU VBUS ordering comparison

The earlier host-before-VBUS hypothesis was based on an incomplete reading of the native path and is withdrawn. Actual Android dmesg shows the external MT6379 VBUS regulator enabled at 456.103503, Type-C entering SOURCE at 456.139753, USB role switching to host at 456.147487, MTU3 port-on at 456.150000, SoC PHY/repeater host setup at 456.1587, and xHCI registering at 456.163. The same Kingston then enumerates high-speed at 459.095620 and is identified as 1f75:0903 at 459.246200. XNU also confirms external VBUS before dali_usb_host_launch(). Both paths therefore source VBUS before host setup.

The native MTU3 ssusb_set_vbus() call happens later, but only votes its optional vbus regulator. Live USB0 DT has vusb33-supply and no vbus-supply (live.dts:10836-10847). Physical VBUS is separately connected through extcon's vbus-supply to mt6379-usb-otg-vbus (mt6991.dts:16280-16284,20574-20578). Android regulator_summary shows 16701000.usb0-vbus at 0mV/0mA and MT6379 OTG VBUS at 5000mV with a configured 1400mA limit; xHCI also logs its VBUS supply missing and using a dummy regulator. Do not treat the later regulator vote as the physical source enable.

This removes a staged host-before-VBUS worker from the proposed fixes. The remaining offline comparison is the MT6379/SC8585 source setup, attached-source CC role, USB/OTG status transition, and USB2 PHY/repeater line state. Native captures still lack synchronized DP/DM and raw final ROLE_CTRL; the patch0138 detailed digest is unavailable. No device, network, code, build, or test operation occurred.


## 2026-10-23 — MT6379 source parity and IBUS ADC correction

Native extcon and XNU both select the SC8585 reverse 1:1/manual ACDRV path, program MT6379 OTG output settings, clear CHG_HD_PP7 bit 5 inside test mode, and set CHG_TOP2 bit 0 to enable output. The source differs in its current selector: XNU sets index0 (500mA); the native MT6379 table is 500/800/1100/1400/1700/2000/2300mA and the captured host attach logs 1400mA. The Kingston declares max_power=200, so steady load should fit either limit; startup peak is not measured. This is a bounded current-policy delta, not a confirmed cause.

Correction to the previous telemetry note: local MT6379 ADC source exists at audit/mt6379-adc.c and its binding header. IBUS is channel 5 with IIO_CURRENT type and is used by mt6379-charger.c for its ipeak calculation. The source does not specify direction or behavior during OTG boost, and no saved capture includes an IBUS sample. Keep it as a possible read-only measurement to validate, not as established USB connector current. XNU's current sampler still records VBUS voltage only. The exact smallest useful device capture would correlate PMIF CC/ROLE_CTRL/OTG/fault, MT6379 IBUS candidate, USB2 DTM/DPDM/repeater registers and xHCI PORTSC over the same attach/source interval. No device, network, code, build, or test operation occurred.


## 2026-10-23 — USB DP/DM telemetry candidates

The local MT6379 ADC binding/driver exposes USBDP channel 2, USBDM channel 7 (voltage), and IBUS channel 5 (current). It uses one-shot conversions and the charger code uses DP/DM for voltage checks; this yields static samples, not a high-speed USB waveform. None appears in saved native attached-host captures. Android's saved voltage capture only reports CHGVIN/PDVBUS. XNU patch0138 reads SoC U2 PHY USB20_GPIO_DP/DM_RO at offset 0x324 after scan, but its detailed output is missing and the vendor XS-PHY source only defines GPIO_CTL/GPIO_MODE controls without local call sites; the validity of that sample without setting those controls is unknown. A physical discriminator should synchronize TCPC ROLE_CTRL/CC/fault, PMIC ADC channels 2/5/7, SoC U2 DTM1/ACR6/repeater state, IPPC OTG status and xHCI PORTSC. ## 2026-10-23 — Android OTG switch-on confirms USB host enumeration

Before switch-on, read-only wireless ADB checks found no disk (`sm list-disks` empty), no `/sys/bus/usb/devices` entries and `debug.device.usb_state=0`. After the user enabled OTG with the same DataTraveler still connected, Android reports `host_connected=true`, port0 connected in DFP/source/host mode, and the USB host manager enumerates Google/DataTraveler VID:PID `1f75:0903`. Vold reports disk `disk:8,48`, size `127951437824` bytes, block device `sdd`, and a public volume with `fsType=hfsplus`, label `iOSRoot`, UUID `DFFB057492EE024A`, state `UNMOUNTABLE`. Thus the OTG toggle, cable/stick and Android host path are confirmed; Android detects the HFS+ disk but cannot mount the volume. `sys.usb.state=adb` remains the phone's gadget-side debug function and is independent of the host result. This makes the XNU `CCS=0`, RxDetect, DP=0/DM=0 result a controller/PHY/Type-C role-transition issue specific to the XNU path, not a missing disk or OTG-switch prerequisite. The exact electrical divergence remains unproven; next compare the live native host role/setup against XNU offline. No settings, partitions, or media were modified.

## 2026-10-23 — XNU replug behavior matches one-shot OTG teardown

User reports that during XNU startup the U-disk LED blinks a few times and then stays lit; unplugging/reinserting while XNU is running leaves it dark, while reinserting under Android lights it again. Source review confirms the no-light reinsert path: `DaliUSBPower::tickLocked()` calls `finishLocked("detached")` and returns false when CC no longer reports the attached partner (`research/dali-usb-root/ci-artifacts/expected-DaliUSBPower-0128.cpp:406-408`). Cleanup stops the host worker, disables MT6379 OTG source, restores the saved TCPC role, and sets state to `Finished` (`:253-323`); future ticks return false (`:326-328`). The PMIC service reschedules its timer only when `source.tick()` returns true (`xnu/patches/0096-dali-bounded-usb-source.patch:294-298`), so there is no automatic hotplug re-arm. Patch0128's longer source budget does not change this. This is a source-confirmed explanation for the second symptom, distinct from the unresolved initial attach/enumeration failure. The user clarifies the LED flashes only during the DALI CP stage and stops blinking during the later host phase. CP setup precedes MT6379 OTG VBUS enable, and xHCI launches only after VBUS is confirmed (`expected-DaliUSBPower-0128.cpp:237-249, 412-423`), so these flashes are power/CP startup activity, not evidence of disk reads. The lack of later blinking supports no sustained storage I/O, but the LED alone is not a protocol trace; the three PORTSC samples were taken before `integratedStorage()` starts the xHCI/BOT scan (`expected-DaliUSBHost-0138.cpp:1217-1233`), so CCS=0/PLS=5 proves only the pre-scan state and does not rule out a later transient attempt. DP/DM=0 is still a clue, but the GPIO read validity is unproven. Confirmation requires the complete XNU lines `DALI USB: device=... BOT`, `DALI MSC: ready ...`, `DALI ROOT: HFS volume ...`, and `DALI ROOT: read-only USB partition available`. Next candidate should add controlled detach cleanup/re-arm and retain these stage markers, while preserving bounded power/current policy; no further tiny trial. No device setting or partition was changed.
